## Phase 8 — Optimization, QA and Release

#### T-8.01 — Critical CSS and asset pipeline — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11, inlining added 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **two criteria still not met and not claimed: the 50 KB target, and fingerprinting**
- **Objective:** Meet the CSS budget from T-1.08.
- **Files affected:** `webpack.config.js`, `postcss.config.js`, `tailwind.config.js`
- **Twilight components:** build pipeline · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** all page tasks
- **Acceptance criteria:** Above-fold CSS inlined, remainder deferred. Unused Tailwind purged without stripping dynamically-generated classes. Assets fingerprinted. Budget met.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **CI WAS RED WHEN THIS TASK OPENED, AND THAT IS THE FIRST FACT.** `app.css` measured **100.2 KB gzip against a 100 KB ceiling** — Phase 7 had grown it past the line T-1.08 drew. It now measures **58.8 KB**, and the build is green.
  - **THE SHEET WAS ANALYSED BEFORE IT WAS TOUCHED, AND THE ANALYSIS IS WHY NOTHING WAS PURGED.** Of 779.5 KB raw, **515 KB is `s-*`** — the classes Salla's own web components render and expect the theme to have styled, kept alive through the purge by `safe-list-css.txt` in Tailwind's `content`. Of their **1860 distinct names, only 79 are defined in the CSS `@salla.sa/twilight-components` ships** — 55 in the runtime entry bundles. **The other 1781 exist nowhere else.** Deleting them does not slim the theme; it un-styles the platform's cart, checkout, filters and reviews on a live store, silently, at runtime. So «purged without stripping dynamically-generated classes» is met by **keeping every one of them** — the criterion's own caveat is the answer to it, not an exception to work around.
  - **What changed is when they arrive.** `scripts/split-css.mjs` lifts them into `salla-components.css` — **364 KB raw, 44.7 KB gzip, 2513 rules** — which `master.twig` loads non-blocking. **Deferring it is safe for a specific reason rather than a hopeful one:** every `salla-*` element is an empty custom element until `twilight.js` hydrates it, so this CSS has nothing to paint during first paint, and CSS for markup that does not yet exist cannot shift a layout. That was the CLS question, and it had to be answered before the deferral was allowed.
  - **THE OUTPUT IS CUT, NOT COMPILED TWICE, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT PROVED WHY.** A second Tailwind pass over the safe-list alone fails outright: the plugin's `s-*` components `@apply` classes **the theme's own SCSS defines** — `single-order-header-item`, from `04-components/user-pages.scss` — and `@apply` can only reach what is in the same PostCSS document. One compile, then a cut, is the only order that works. Recorded because the two-config version looks obviously right and is not.
  - **Cascade order is preserved by linking the deferred sheet FIRST in the document**, which reads like a typo and is the opposite. CSSOM order follows document order, not load order, so this reproduces exactly the order the two halves had inside one file. **The files that deliberately override an `s-*` class still win** — `floating-menu.scss` styles `s-user-menu-*`, `notifications.scss` styles `s-notification-*` — because they sit below the marker and never move. That is the sharpest test in the file: a cascade bug no byte count would reveal.
  - **The marker is a build guard, not a convention.** If `__THEME_CSS_STARTS_HERE__` ever goes missing the splitter **exits 1 and writes nothing**, rather than shipping one unsplit 100 KB sheet with nothing to explain it. It runs from a webpack `afterEmit` hook so `pnpm watch` produces the same two sheets a production build does — a developer debugging a stale single `app.css` would be reading the wrong file.
  - **⚠ THE 50 KB TARGET IS NOT MET. `app.css` is 58.8 KB, and the gap is real rather than rounding.** What remains is 180 KB of Tailwind utilities the theme's own markup uses and **235 KB of the theme's own SCSS** — every page's components in one sheet. Meeting 50 KB means route-splitting that 235 KB so a product page stops loading the loyalty, orders and checkout CSS. **That is a real change with a real design, and it was not smuggled in under a task about the pipeline.** Flagged for the owner; the budget checker prints «above target» rather than passing quietly.
  - **A measurement I got wrong mid-task, corrected here because the number was quoted:** a first probe suggested 94 KB raw of utilities were referenced only by the safe-list and could move. It used `some()` where the splitter correctly requires `every()`, and counted `s-*` rules already moved. **The true additional saving from that rule is about 1 KB**, and it is in the splitter because it is correct, not because it is large.
  - **⚠ «Assets fingerprinted» is not done, and cannot be done by the theme.** Entry filenames are referenced literally in Twig — `{{ 'app.css' | asset }}` — so a `[contenthash]` on entries breaks every reference; there is no manifest indirection a Twig template can read. Async chunks already carry `[contenthash]`, which is safe because webpack resolves those itself. **Whether the platform fingerprints theme assets at the CDN is undocumented** — the Master Layout page does not say — so it is a question for Salla rather than a claim in either direction.
  - **What became visible:** every page stops blocking first paint on 44.7 KB of CSS for components that have not loaded yet.
  - 13 tests, the sharpest being that a theme override of an `s-*` class below the marker never moves.

- **What was done — second pass, 2026-08-12, «above-fold CSS inlined»:**
  - **THE OWNER RULED THE FOLD, AND THE RULING IS WHY THIS COULD PROCEED.** The first pass stopped because «which rules are above the fold» had no answer without a rendered page. The answer given on 2026-08-12 is **the announcement bar, the header AND the hero** — not the header alone, because the hero is a full-bleed image directly beneath the bar and an unstyled one is a flash of unstyled content in the first thing a visitor sees. That ruling is now the `SURFACE` list in `scripts/extract-critical.mjs`.
  - **⚠ THE RULES ARE DERIVED FROM THE TEMPLATES, NOT OBSERVED IN A BROWSER, AND THAT IS A SUBSTITUTION WORTH STATING.** There is no browser in this repository — no Chromium, no Playwright, only `jsdom`, which parses markup and never lays anything out. The script reads the above-fold Twig files plus `sticky-header.js`, and keeps every rule that could match them. **The error runs in the safe direction:** a browser reports the *minimum* set and anything it missed is absent, so the page paints wrong; static matching produces a *superset*, at every breakpoint and in every state. Over-inclusion costs bytes; under-inclusion costs a wrong first frame. Recorded as **AC-12**.
  - **`master.twig` now blocks on no stylesheet at all** except the two fonts. **4.9 KB inlined**, `app.css` (58.8 KB) and `salla-components.css` (44.7 KB) both fetched non-blocking, each with a render-blocking `<noscript>` copy — without which a JavaScript-less visitor gets a store with 4.9 KB of header CSS and nothing else.
  - **The inline block comes FIRST of the three, and that is the correctness argument rather than a preference.** Every rule in it is a verbatim copy of a rule in `app.css`, and `app.css` still loads last — so any pair of rules the extract split apart resolves exactly as it did in one file. Put the `<style>` below the link and every kept rule starts beating the rules written to override it. Asserted, not just commented.
  - **`critical.css` gets its own budget row, and its ceiling is a different kind of number.** 14 KB — the first-round-trip figure, and the point past which inlining stops being cheaper than a request. Unlike every other row it is paid on **every page view and never cached**.
  - **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY THE TESTS, BOTH OF WHICH KEPT THE SHEET LOOKING RIGHT WHILE BEING WRONG.** They are recorded because each one produced a plausible file rather than an error. **(1)** The selector list was split on bare commas — but `postcss-preset-env` compiles this theme's logical properties into `:where([dir=rtl],[dir=rtl] *)`, whose comma is *inside* the pseudo-class. The fragments had no class in them, read as base rules, and were kept: the footer, the story cards and the product page all rode in. **(2)** «No class means base rule, keep it» kept `lite-youtube{…}`, dragging the video-carousel façade in on a rule meant for `html` and `body`. **29.3 KB → 19.7 KB raw → 4.9 KB gzip** across the two fixes.
  - **⚠ WHETHER THE PAGE ACTUALLY PAINTS CORRECTLY IS NOT PROVEN AND IS NOT CLAIMED.** The suite proves the extract is a faithful, ordered subset containing the bar, the header and the hero. Only a browser can say the first frame is right. **`/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §3.4** is that check — Home on a throttled phone, watched for a flash, then with JavaScript off, then with the bar disabled and the hero removed — and it says explicitly that the fix for a miss is to name the missing surface in the script, never to re-inline `app.css`.
  - **What became visible:** the store's first frame — bar, header and hero — now paints from the HTML document, with no stylesheet round trip in front of it.
  - 24 further tests. The sharpest is that every kept block appears **verbatim** in `app.css`, which is what makes the cascade argument true; the most useful is the one asserting the extract is under 15% of the sheet, because an extractor that keeps everything passes every «is it there?» test in the file.

#### T-8.02 — Image strategy audit — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11, responsive pass 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **measured CLS is T-8.08's and needs a browser**
- **Objective:** Enforce doc 11's image rules everywhere.
- **Files affected:** all templates
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** image fit type
- **Dependencies:** all page tasks
- **Acceptance criteria:** Every image has explicit dimensions or aspect-ratio. Modern formats served with fallback. Only the LCP image is eager. Measured CLS at or near zero on every template.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **ALL 41 `<img>` TAGS IN `src/views` WERE READ, AND EVERY ONE IN A TEMPLATE THIS THEME WROTE ALREADY PASSED.** There was no pile of defects. The box is reserved, the loading strategy is deliberate, and the only eager image on a page is the one whose LCP it is. **So the deliverable is the thing that keeps it true** — `scripts/check-images.mjs`, wired into `pnpm lint` as `lint:images`.
  - **The box is reserved two ways and the checker honours both.** `width`+`height` on the tag is one. The other is an ancestor whose SCSS sets `aspect-ratio`, which is how every media well here does it — a merchant's image has no dimensions a template can know. `.card__media` carries `--card-media-ratio`, `.hero__image` 5/7, `.hotspot__image` 3/4, `.listing-cover__image` 393/420, `.image-slider .swiper-slide` `--pdp-media-ratio`. **Demanding attributes on those would push false dimensions onto a responsive box**, which is a defect dressed as compliance.
  - **«Only the LCP image is eager» is per page, and that is how it is enforced.** Home's hero, the PDP's first gallery frame and the header's logo are each correct where they are; all three sliders mark `loop.first` eager and everything after it lazy. What the check catches is **the second one** — a new section marking its image eager and quietly competing with the LCP for bandwidth.
  - **Two exemptions, each a measurement fact rather than a concession.** An image inside a `salla-modal` or `<dialog>` is exempt, because CLS counts movement of *rendered* content and a closed modal renders nothing — the footer's tax certificate is the case, a merchant document whose dimensions no template can know. And **11 upstream templates the theme has never adopted are skipped and listed by name**, because editing one means shadowing it and buying `/docs/OVERRIDES.md` a row carried through every SDK upgrade. `blog/*` is on that list twice over: B6 ruled the blog out, so it has no route at all.
  - **The ancestor test is a heuristic, and the script says so in its own comments.** It asks whether a reserving class appears earlier in the template, which cannot tell an ancestor from a sibling that merely came first. It errs toward silence, catching the regression that matters — a new template with a bare `<img>` and no reservation anywhere. The precise version needs a Twig parser, a larger dependency than this check is worth.
  - **⚠ «Modern formats served with fallback» is the CDN's, not the theme's.** Images go through `|cdn(...)` and format negotiation happens on Salla's side from the request's `Accept` header. **A theme cannot emit a `<picture>` for a URL it does not control**, so this is recorded as platform behaviour rather than claimed as built — and it is on the support list, because no document confirms it.
  - **⚠ «Measured CLS at or near zero on every template» is not done here.** This task proves the boxes are *reserved*; only a browser proves nothing *moved*. It belongs to T-8.08 and is on the manual checklist.
  - **What became visible:** nothing changed on screen — an image that never shifted still does not. What changed is that the next one cannot regress silently.
  - 11 tests, and most of them feed the checker broken markup: **a check that cannot fail is the worst outcome of an audit task.**

- **Second pass, 2026-08-12 — «srcset where possible», and a written record of where it was not:**
  - **THE AUDIT FIRST, AND IT FOUND ONE GAP RATHER THAN MANY.** All 42 `<img>` tags in `src/views` were re-read against the three rules. Reserved boxes, `alt` and the loading strategy were already clean — that is what the first pass built and what CI has been holding. **Zero of the 42 carried a `srcset`**, and only three used `|cdn()` at all. So the gap was responsive candidates, and nothing else.
  - **`|cdn()` WORKS ON A MERCHANT SETTING'S URL, AND THAT IS PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `header.twig` has always piped `theme.settings.get('logo_light')` — a full URL from a theme setting — through `|cdn(175)`. Every responsive image in this pass is the same class of value, so the filter that builds a second URL at another width was already known to work on it.
  - **Seven tags gained candidates.** Fluid boxes get `w`-descriptors and a `sizes`: the hero and the partner banner and the hotspot are `.container`, so `(min-width: 1536px) 1280px, calc(100vw - 32px)`; the offers banner is full-bleed, so `100vw`; the story card is a `column-count` grid of 2/3/4, so `(min-width: 1024px) 22vw, (min-width: 768px) 30vw, 44vw`. **Every one of those was read off the stylesheet, not guessed.** The two logos and the tax mark are fixed boxes and get `1x`/`2x` instead — `sizes` on a fixed box is noise, and `w`-descriptors ask the browser to solve a problem it does not have.
  - **⚠ THE PRELOAD WAS THE TRAP, AND IT IS THE SHARPEST THING IN THIS PASS.** The hero is Home's LCP and T-4.05 preloads it. **A `rel="preload"` with a bare `href` fetches that one URL** — so the moment the tag offered a candidate list, every viewport whose winner was not `href` would download **two** hero images and preload the one it did not use. An optimisation that makes T-8.08's measurement worse while looking like an improvement. The preload now carries `imagesrcset` and `imagesizes`, and a test pins the two width lists together.
  - **⚠ ONE THEME-OWNED IMAGE IS DELIBERATELY LEFT WITHOUT ONE, WITH THE REASON IN THE SCRIPT.** The video carousel's cover is a `salla-slider type="carousel" centered` child: its width is decided by the platform component at runtime and appears in no stylesheet this repository owns. **A wrong `sizes` is worse than none** — it makes the browser pick a candidate for a box that is not the box, permanently and invisibly. It is listed in `SRCSET_DEFERRED` and printed on every run rather than quietly skipped.
  - **⚠ TWELVE IMAGES ARE NOT THE THEME'S TO FIX, AND THE SCRIPT NOW SAYS SO BY NAME.** Product galleries, cart lines, order attachments, the shipping carrier's logo, a merchant's tax certificate, and the three `{image}` slots `salla-payments` fills at runtime. The theme re-presents a URL it is handed. **`|cdn()` was not applied to any of them on the guess that it would work**: a filter that mangles a URL replaces a correct image with a broken one, and it would break on a live store rather than in CI. Resizing those is Salla's, at the CDN.
  - **The checker reports, and does not fail, on all three of these.** The rules it enforces are the theme's to keep; whether an image *can* carry a `srcset` depends on who owns the URL, and a lint script must not fail a build over the platform's answer.
  - **What became visible:** a phone downloads a hero sized for a phone.
  - 18 further tests. `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` **§3.6** carries what none of them can: that Salla's CDN honours `|cdn(450)` at all, that the hero is fetched **once**, that a 2× screen gets the 2× logo, and that `currentSrc` matches the box at three viewports.

#### T-8.03 — JavaScript code-splitting — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11**
- **Objective:** Meet the JS budget.
- **Files affected:** `webpack.config.js`, `src/assets/js/*`
- **Twilight components:** all JS modules · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** all page tasks
- **Acceptance criteria:** Feature-based chunks per doc 15. Customer-only code loads after authentication. No duplicated logic across bundles. Budget met.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **THE FAILING CRITERION WAS «NO DUPLICATED LOGIC», AND IT WAS FAILING INVISIBLY.** A `--json` build measured **60.3 KB raw duplicated across bundles** — separate webpack entries do not share modules, and nothing in the build output said so. It is now **7.5 KB**, and what remains is named below rather than rounded away.
  - **`fslightbox` and `lite-youtube-embed` were 41 KB of it, compiled into both `home.js` and `product.js`.** A customer who opened Home and then a product page downloaded both libraries twice. They now live in `js/vendor/media.js`, a shared entry both bundles `dependOn`, loaded by `index.twig` and `product/single.twig` before their own bundle.
  - **It is deliberately NOT in `app.js`.** Every page loads that bundle, and a customer reading the shipping policy has no lightbox and no video: moving 41 KB onto every page to save it on two is the wrong direction. Same reasoning that kept the stories feed out of the Home bundle in T-7.01.
  - **`window.fslightbox` is still assigned by the pages, not by the shared module.** Upstream markup reaches for that global, and moving the assignment would change *when* it appears relative to code expecting it. The module exports; the pages publish.
  - **The theme's own shared modules were deduplicated by `dependOn: 'app'`** — `base-page.js` was in **six** chunks, `product-runtime.js` in three. `app.js` loads on every page and is `defer`red before the page-scripts block, so a reference into it always resolves.
  - **⚠ THREE BUNDLES DELIBERATELY DO NOT `dependOn` app, AND GETTING THIS WRONG WOULD BREAK EVERY PAGE.** `product-card.js`, `main-menu.js` and `add-product-toast.js` load in `<head>`, **before `app.js` is parsed**. A shared-module reference into app would be a reference into a bundle that has not run. Pinned by a test.
  - **The result on the two pages that carry the most traffic:** `home.js` fell from **42.0 KB raw to 6.96 KB**, `product.js` from **42.7 KB to 7.72 KB**. Total JS in the build fell from 256 KB to 221 KB. `app.js` grew 1 KB, absorbing the shared modules that were previously copied six ways.
  - **⚠ 7.5 KB of duplication remains and is a measured trade, not an oversight.** `hotspots.js` (5.7 KB) and `story-modal.js` (1.7 KB) are in both `home.js` and `stories.js` — the only two surfaces with a story feed. Extracting them means a third shared bundle and a third `<script>` on both pages; **at ~2 KB gzip, the extra request costs more than the duplication.** Folding them into `app.js` would put them on every page instead, which is worse.
  - **«Customer-only code loads after authentication» is met by the page, not by a guard.** `order.js`, `notifications.js` and `pages.js` are loaded only by the customer-area templates, which the platform serves only to a signed-in customer. Nothing in `app.js` is customer-only.
  - **A defect found and fixed on the way:** T-8.01's splitter wrote its banner to **stdout**, which corrupts `webpack --json` — the very command this task needed. It writes to stderr now.
  - **What became visible:** Home and the product page each ship about 35 KB less JavaScript.
  - 6 tests, the sharpest being that each template loads `media.js` *before* its own bundle — `defer` runs in document order, and the wrong order throws at runtime.

#### T-8.04 — Structured data — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-11, `ItemList` added 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **Rich Results validation is manual**
- **Objective:** Product, BreadcrumbList, Organization, FAQPage schema.
- **Files affected:** `master.twig`, product, FAQ, brand templates
- **Twilight components:** `salla-metadata`, `salla-breadcrumb`
- **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** product, breadcrumb, store · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-4.10, T-7.02
- **Acceptance criteria:** Validates in Google Rich Results with zero errors. No schema for content absent from the page.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **Two of the four nodes already existed; two did not.** `Product` shipped with T-4.10, `FAQPage` with T-7.02, and upstream's brand page carries a `Brand`. **`Organization` and `BreadcrumbList` were missing**, and those are what this task added.
  - **`Organization` goes in the layout, because it is the same fact on every page** and one node per document is what search engines expect. It is the only site-wide node; every other one is emitted by the page that owns the content.
  - **Every field is guarded, which is «no schema for content absent from the page» applied inside a node.** A store with no logo emits no `logo` key; one with neither phone nor email emits no `contactPoint`. **An empty string in structured data is worse than an absent key** — it is a positive claim that the value is empty, and it is the kind of thing that gets rich results withdrawn.
  - **`sameAs` is deliberately absent.** It wants the store's social profile URLs, and `salla-social` resolves those **client-side** from data no Twig variable exposes. Writing the key with nothing in it would be the empty claim the rule above forbids.
  - **⚠ THE PRODUCT PAGE IS THE ONLY PAGE THAT CAN CARRY A `BreadcrumbList`, AND THE OTHERS THEREFORE CARRY NONE.** `<salla-breadcrumb>` builds its trail **client-side** from `salla.api.navigation.fetchBreadcrumbs`; no Twig variable carries a trail on any page. The exception is `product.category`, which gives the one hop that matters — home › category › product. **A trail the template cannot see is not a trail it may guess**, so every other page emits nothing rather than something invented.
  - **The last breadcrumb item carries no `item` URL**, which is Google's own guidance rather than an omission: the reader is already on that page.
  - **`json_encode|raw` everywhere, never interpolation.** A store whose name contains a quote — or a product name with a Unicode line separator — would otherwise emit JSON that does not parse, **and a node that does not parse is silently ignored.** Nothing looks wrong, nothing logs, and the rich result disappears weeks later. That is the failure this task's tests are built around: each node is rendered with representative values and **parsed as JSON**, including a store called `Sam's "Shop"` and a bare install that has set almost nothing.
  - **⚠ «Validates in Google Rich Results with zero errors» is not done here.** That needs the live URL in Google's validator, which needs a published storefront. It is on the manual checklist with the exact URLs to paste.
  - **What became visible:** nothing on screen — search engines now get the store's identity on every page and the product's place in the catalogue on the PDP.
  - 11 tests, the sharpest being that a bare install with no logo and no contacts still emits JSON that parses.

- **Second pass, 2026-08-12 — `ItemList` on the listing pages, and an audit of the four:**
  - **⚠ TWO PREMISES IN THE INSTRUCTION WERE WRONG AND ARE CORRECTED RATHER THAN ACTED ON.** The instruction was that three of the four objective types exist, and that `FAQPage` and `Offer` should be recorded as available options **not taken**. **All four exist, and both of those are taken.** `FAQPage` ships in `components/home/faq.twig` — built with `|map(item => …)|filter(…)|values` and single-quoted keys, which is why a grep for `"@type": "FAQPage"` misses it — and `Offer` ships nested in the `Product` node with `url`, `price`, `priceCurrency` and `availability`. **Recording either as not-taken would have put a false statement in the register that exists to prevent exactly that.** What was recorded instead is what was verified: **AC-14**.
  - **`Article` stays refused.** B6 ruled it out and nothing changed: the stories are a theme setting rendered as a modal over the feed, not a page and not a route, so there is no article and no URL to describe.
  - **⚠ `ItemList` CANNOT BE SERVER-RENDERED HERE, AND THAT IS A FACT ABOUT THE PAGE RATHER THAN A CHOICE.** `pages/product/index.twig` receives `page`, `category`, `filters`, `sort_options` and `search_query` — **and no product collection at all**. The grid is `<salla-products-list source="…">`, which fetches over the API after load. So the node is built in the browser from the **rendered cards**, which is also what keeps it honest: it cannot disagree with what the visitor is looking at after a filter, a sort, or the next page of an infinite scroll.
  - **Why that is not the `BreadcrumbList` case this task already answered by emitting nothing.** There the trail existed nowhere, in Twig or the DOM, so any trail would have been **invented** — «a trail the template cannot see is not a trail it may guess». Here the products are real, present and visible; they arrive a moment later. **Describing what is on the page is not guessing at what is not.**
  - **The two negative rules carry the design.** An empty result emits **no node at all** rather than an `ItemList` claiming zero items — the same «an empty claim is worse than an absent key» rule this task applied to `sameAs` and `contactPoint`. And a half-rendered card with no name or URL is skipped rather than described as blank.
  - **One genuine gap found in the existing four and fixed:** the `Product` node emitted a **single** `image` where the page has a whole gallery. Google's guidance asks for every image of the item, and `product.images` was already there — the same collection the gallery renders. It falls back to the single image when the collection is absent, so a one-photo product is unchanged.
  - **⚠ THIS NODE IS A WEAKER GUARANTEE THAN THE OTHER FOUR AND IS RECORDED AS ONE.** Google executes JavaScript before extracting structured data, so the arrangement is supported rather than a trick — but it is second-pass rendering, it can be delayed, and no crawler is obliged to run scripts. **Whether it is seen at all is `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §1.6**, which also says what to do if it is not: record it and take it to Salla as a question about server-rendering the grid, **not** move it into Twig, where there is no data to move.
  - **What became visible:** nothing on screen — a category page now tells search engines which products it is listing, and in what order.
  - 11 further tests, run against a real DOM. The sharpest is that a listing which empties out **removes** the node it had, rather than leaving the previous filter's products described in the head.

#### T-8.05 — Metadata and canonicals — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-12 with no metadata added; robots ruled out 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **all four criteria are the platform's and are on the manual checklist**
- **Objective:** Titles, descriptions, canonicals, robots, Open Graph, Twitter Card.
- **Files affected:** `master.twig`, all page templates
- **Twilight components:** `salla-metadata`
- **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** page metadata · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-8.04
- **Acceptance criteria:** Unique title and description per template. Canonicals correct on filtered and paginated URLs. OG images resolve. Arabic metadata renders correctly in previews.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **⚠ THE THEME EMITS NO METADATA, AND THAT IS THE ANSWER RATHER THAN THE OMISSION.** Upstream `theme-raed` at the pinned `1.365.0` tag contains **no `<title>`, no `rel="canonical"`, no meta description, no `og:*` and no `twitter:*` in any file** — verified with `git grep` against the tag, not from memory. It is a published theme running on thousands of live stores with working titles, so the platform must be injecting them. Salla's own master-layout documentation says the head hooks are «responsible for adding the SEO-related meta data to the page header section». **Adding these tags here would produce a second title and a second canonical competing with the platform's, which is worse than none** — the crawler picks one, and not necessarily ours.
  - **`salla-metadata` is not what this row assumed.** The backlog names it as this task's Twilight component. It is not an SEO element: it renders the merchant's **custom product fields** in the page body, and `product/single.twig` already uses it that way behind `product.has_metadata`. The row is left as written and corrected here rather than silently edited.
  - **What the task adds instead is a guard against the two ways this arrangement breaks**, both of which are silent on a live store and invisible in a template diff. `tests/t-8.05-metadata.test.mjs`, 13 tests: **a head hook going missing** — `master.twig` is a technique-A shadow, and dropping `{% hook head %}` in an unrelated edit deletes the title, description, canonical and OG tags from every page at once with nothing to see locally — and **a template starting to emit its own**, which is how a future task «adding SEO tags» in good faith lands a duplicate. Also asserted: all three hooks sit inside `<head>` and in order, and there is exactly **one** document shell, so a second layout cannot quietly appear with no hooks in it.
  - **The one part of «canonicals correct on filtered URLs» that lives in this repository is asserted, not assumed.** Three of the theme's own scripts — `products.js`, `testimonials.js`, `sort-disclosure.js` — rewrite the address bar with `history.pushState`. A test requires every one of those calls to go through `salla.helpers.addParamToUrl`, so they can only ever add a **query parameter**. A script that pushed a new **path** would mint a URL the platform never rendered and that therefore has no metadata behind it at all.
  - **The tests were mutation-checked rather than trusted.** Four defects were planted — a dropped `{% hook head %}`, a theme-side `<title>`, an `og:title` and a `rel="canonical"` — and six assertions failed. One assertion was found vacuous while doing it and fixed: `a < b < c` is legal JavaScript meaning `(a < b) < c`, which compares a boolean against an index and is true for every input. The reason is written above the fix so it is not reintroduced.
  - **⚠ ALL FOUR ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA BELONG TO THE PLATFORM AND NONE IS CLAIMED HERE.** Uniqueness per template, canonicals on filtered and paginated URLs, OG images resolving, and Arabic rendering in previews are all properties of tags this theme does not emit, on a storefront this repository cannot open. They are written out in full as **`/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §1** — eleven URLs to open, the exact controls to operate, and a stated failure condition for each. §1.2 (canonical on a sorted URL) and §1.4 (Arabic bidi in preview cards) are the two most likely to actually fail.
  - **A standing rule comes out of this and is recorded in the checklist:** where a manual check fails, **the fix is not a theme change.** Adding the missing tag to a template creates the duplicate this task exists to prevent. It is a store-settings or platform conversation.
  - **What became visible:** nothing on screen — the arrangement that was already correct is now one a future edit cannot break quietly.

- **Robots ruled out, 2026-08-12 by the project owner, and the four criteria re-verified rather than re-implemented:**
  - **⚠ «Implement the four criteria» cannot mean emitting the tags, and this is the finding rather than the work.** The criteria are unique titles and descriptions, correct canonicals, and OG images that resolve. **The theme emits none of those tags on purpose** — upstream `theme-raed` at `1.365.0` contains no `<title>`, no `rel="canonical"`, no description and no `og:*` in any file, and Salla injects them through the head hooks. Adding them now produces **a second title and a second canonical competing with the platform's**, which is the exact defect this task's 13 tests exist to catch, and which the task already recorded as a standing rule: **where a manual check fails, the fix is not a theme change.**
  - **So what was done is verification, and it was worth doing.** `master.twig` was edited four times today — the critical-CSS block, the announcement dismissal script, nine colour custom properties, then four more. **All three head hooks are still present, still inside `<head>`, and still in order**, and no template has started emitting a competing tag. That is precisely the regression the guard was built for, and it is the first time it has had real churn to survive.
  - **`robots` is declined, and the reasoning is recorded as AC-15.** It appears in this task's **objective** line and in none of its **acceptance criteria**, and the task's scope is its criteria. Two reasons: indexing is an **operational** decision — which store is live, which is staging — that belongs to whoever knows the answer, never a template. And **a forgotten `noindex` on a live store fails silently and totally**: the merchant sees a published storefront, nothing errors, and the store is invisible to Google, possibly for months. **A control the theme cannot get wrong is better than one it can.**
  - **⚠ The risk that ruling leaves open is named rather than waved away.** Whether Salla blocks preview and demo storefronts at the platform level **is not known**, and was not assumed in either direction. `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §1.5.1 is the check — View Source on the demo store, then `site:` it in Google — and it says plainly that **the theme cannot see, cause or fix a demo store competing with the real one**, and that the answer is needed **before** the first publish, because removing an indexed duplicate is far slower than never creating one.
  - **A «Questions for Salla» table was opened in `/docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md`.** Five questions this repository cannot answer were scattered across three documents — CDN fingerprinting, the picker's Hijri locale, server-rendering the products grid, demo-store indexing, and whether the platform injects `twitter:card` at all. **None is a theme defect**; each is an undocumented platform fact that blocks or shapes a decision, and they were impossible to act on while they lived in five different paragraphs.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — a tag the theme was asked to add is now recorded as one it must never add, with the reason.

#### T-8.06 — Accessibility audit, WCAG 2.1 AA — ⚠ **PARTLY DONE 2026-08-12 — the automated third is built and green; the audit itself is NOT done and is not claimed**
- **Objective:** Conformance across every screen.
- **Files affected:** all
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** all page tasks
- **Acceptance criteria:** Keyboard-only pass on every flow. Screen reader pass in Arabic with RTL focus order verified. Contrast validated against final tokens. Automated scan clean; manual findings triaged and fixed. Doc 13 checklist signed.
- **Complexity:** L
- **What was done — and read the status line before the detail: this task is open.**
  - **Of the five criteria, one is met, one is met in part, and three cannot be touched from here.** «Automated scan clean» ✅. «Contrast validated against final tokens» ✅ **for the token layer** and ⚠ **not for text over merchant images**, which is the case a machine cannot see. The keyboard pass, the screen-reader pass in Arabic and the signing of doc 13's checklist **need a person at a browser** and are not claimed.
  - **`scripts/check-a11y.mjs`, wired into `pnpm lint` and into CI as its own step.** BUDGETS.md §1 already said automated checking tops out at about a third of WCAG; the script says so too, in its own failure output, so nobody reads a green run as conformance.
  - **§1 recomputes contrast from the tokens rather than trusting the table.** `/docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md` carries 18 pairs computed by hand under T-2.01 and recomputed under T-2.17. A table is a claim about values on the day it was written. The script reads the hexes out of `01-settings/global.scss`, resolves the `var()` aliases — `--border-interactive` and `--surface-control` are both aliases of `--text-secondary`, and a checker that could not follow one would skip the two tokens most likely to be edited — and computes WCAG 2.1 relative luminance. **All 18 recompute to exactly the documented figure**, which is an independent confirmation of the T-2.17 table rather than a restatement of it. Each pair also carries its threshold and its criterion, so a failure says *which rule* broke. The two pairs the documentation **exempts** are still pinned to their recorded numbers: an exemption is an argument about a specific value and it expires when the value moves.
  - **§2 is a static scan of all 75 templates** — missing `alt`, controls with no accessible name, positive `tabindex`, `aria-hidden` on a focusable element, misspelled `aria-*` and unknown `role`. **The theme passes today with nothing to fix**: every `<img>` already has `alt`, every link and button already has a name. So the deliverable is a ratchet, not a repair, and the entry says so rather than implying a clean-up happened.
  - **Two parsing traps were hit while writing it, and both are recorded in the script because the next person will hit them too.** **Twig comments must be blanked, not deleted** — this repository's `{# … #}` blocks discuss markup at length, and the first draft reported two `<img>` tags that exist only inside prose; blanking preserves newlines so a line number still points at the real line. And **`<img alt>` is an accessible name** — a link wrapping only a logo is named by that image, and stripping tags before looking for text made six correct links look nameless.
  - **The script found two defects on its first run, and both were its own.** It resolved `--focus-ring-color` to white, because the file ends with `.dark { --focus-ring-color: var(--surface-card) }` and a whole-file scan takes the *last* definition; it now reads the `:root` block only. And it flagged `cart.twig`'s media link for carrying `aria-hidden="true"` while being an `<a>` — but that link has `tabindex="-1"`, so it is out of the tab order, which makes hiding the duplicate link **the textbook treatment** rather than a defect. Both fixes carry the reasoning inline.
  - **Mutation-checked, like T-8.05:** six planted defects — a lightened `--text-secondary`, `tabindex="2"`, `aria-lable`, `role="banana"`, an `<img>` with no `alt`, an empty `<a>` — produced fourteen failures across both halves.
  - **⚠ `lint:images` was added to CI here, and it belongs to T-8.02.** That task's title was «the image rules become a check that can fail», but it wired the script only into `pnpm lint`, which **no CI step runs** — so the check could not fail a build. One line, fixed in passing rather than left as a false guarantee, and recorded here rather than slipped in silently. Both whole-repo checks now run every time, for the reason the CI comment gives: an image loses its reserved box when the *stylesheet* changes, and a contrast pair breaks when a *token* moves — neither edit touches the file that fails, so a changed-files ratchet cannot see either.
  - **⚠ THE AUDIT IS `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §2, AND IT IS THE LARGER HALF OF THIS TASK.** Eleven keyboard flows end to end; the same eleven again with VoiceOver or NVDA **speaking Arabic and the screen off**; RTL focus order and bidi; 44px targets; contrast over a deliberately pale merchant image; reflow at 200% and 400%; and doc 13's eight rows signed against that evidence and nothing else. **§2.8 collects what earlier tasks deferred here** so none of it is lost — T-5.02's four cross-origin checkout questions, T-5.03's OTP countdown, T-8.01's un-inlined critical CSS, AC-9, and the fact that **`tests/harness/dom.mjs` deliberately never simulated the focus trap, `Esc`, focus return or inertness**, so §2.1's dialog rows are the first real check those four have ever had.
  - **The likeliest finding is named in advance rather than discovered late.** **F6** — the focus ring cannot cross into a `salla-*` shadow root — is a system-level limit recorded in `/docs/DESIGN-SYSTEM.md` §6, and §2.1 says exactly which five components to check it on and that the fix is technique C or a platform report, never a fork.
  - **What became visible:** nothing on screen — 18 contrast pairs and 75 templates now fail the build instead of drifting.

#### T-8.07 — Reduced-motion pass — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **one known gap recorded, and watching it happen is manual**
- **Objective:** Every animation in doc 14 respects the preference.
- **Files affected:** all SCSS and JS with motion
- **Twilight components:** all animated · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-8.06
- **Acceptance criteria:** With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, no non-essential motion plays and no functionality is lost.
- **Complexity:** S
- **What was done:**
  - **T-2.03 built the mechanism; this task audited it, and the audit found one non-obvious rule worth more than the rest of the work.** The blanket clamp sets every animation to 0.01ms and pins `animation-iteration-count` to **1**. For a **finite** animation that is exactly right. **For an infinite one it is wrong in a way that looks like nothing until you see it: pinning the count to 1 does not stop the animation at its start — it runs one instant cycle and rests at its END state.** A marquee ends with its text scrolled out of the bar; a shimmer ends with its bright band parked wherever 100% put it. **The theme has already been bitten by this twice** — `announcement.scss` and `skeleton.scss` each carry a rule of their own because of it, and neither entry explained that they are two instances of one rule. Now they do.
  - **All four infinite animations in the stylesheets were found and dispositioned, and two of them need no rule.** `announcement-scroll` and `skeleton-sweep` have their own suppression. `header-skel-shimmer` and `loader` are **upstream and safe by end state** — the shimmer runs `background-position` 200% → −200% at `background-size: 200%`, so the gradient sits entirely **off the element at both endpoints** and the end state is the flat grey bed; the spinner ends at 360°, pixel-identical to 0°. **Neither was "fixed", because fixing an upstream file means shadowing it and buying `/docs/OVERRIDES.md` a row — for nothing.** The reasoning is recorded so a later reader does not add a rule that costs an override and buys no behaviour.
  - **The register is the deliverable.** `tests/t-8.07-reduced-motion.test.mjs` fails on an infinite animation with no entry, and fails on an entry whose animation is gone. That is the only way the fifth occurrence of a bug that has already happened twice gets caught before a user finds it.
  - **The sharpest assertion in the suite is about a value that looks like timidity.** `0.01ms` reads like a number somebody was afraid to round down. It is not: **a transition with duration `0s` fires no `transitionend`**, and `wishlist.js` removes the list item from the DOM inside a `transitionend` handler. Round the clamp to zero and, **for reduce-motion users only**, removed wishlist items stay on the page forever — nothing throws, nothing logs. `accordion.js` depends on the same event to unset `hidden`. A test now pins every clamped duration to non-zero and says why.
  - **Also asserted:** the clamp reaches `*`, `*::before` and `*::after` with all six declarations and `!important` — which has to beat inline styles animation libraries write at runtime; the motion tokens collapse; `motion.scss` is still the **last** import in `app.scss`; and **no stylesheet re-enables motion inside a reduce block**, which is an easy contradiction to write by copying a rule.
  - **The JS side was audited too, and it is already correct.** `home.js` reads `prefers-reduced-motion` **live** — initial state from `matches`, plus a `change` listener — so hero and photo-carousel autoplay are **paused on arrival**, not merely pausable, which is more than WCAG 2.2.2 asks. `parallax-background` turned out to animate nothing: `bg-fixed` is cancelled by `background-attachment: initial` on the next line. `app.js` and `product.js`'s intervals are polling, not motion.
  - **⚠ ONE GENUINE GAP, RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED.** Upstream's `add-product-toast` drives its progress bar by writing `style.width` on a 50 ms `setInterval`. **No CSS clamp can reach a JS timer**, so that bar keeps moving under the preference. It is **off by default** — T-2.12 set `enable_add_product_toast` to `false` because the design's toast is the slim `salla.notify` one — and it is an upstream component this theme has never adopted. Fixing it means adopting it. **The honest outcome is that enabling that setting is a known reduced-motion failure**, checked in T-8.10 when every setting is toggled, and a reason to leave it off.
  - **Mutation-checked:** five planted defects — a `0s` clamp, a reduce block setting a 2 s duration, an unregistered infinite animation, a stale register entry, and `motion.scss` losing its last position — produced six failures.
  - **⚠ Nothing in the suite has ever seen motion stop.** jsdom applies no stylesheets and runs no animations; these are assertions about the *shape* of the suppression. **`/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §2.9** is the check that watches it — the OS setting rather than the DevTools override, so the platform's own components see it too, and eleven rows covering doc 14's nine animations plus the marquee and the shimmer, each with the end-state failure named.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — the marquee and the shimmer already stopped correctly. What changed is that the fifth infinite animation cannot be added without saying what happens to it.

#### T-8.08 — Core Web Vitals verification — ⚠ **NOT DONE — prepared 2026-08-12, and it cannot be done from this repository at all**
- **Objective:** Prove the T-1.08 budgets on real templates.
- **Files affected:** none (measurement)
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-8.01 → T-8.03
- **Acceptance criteria:** LCP, CLS and INP within budget on Home, PDP and Cart, measured on throttled mobile, not desktop.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done — nothing was measured, and nothing is claimed. This entry records the preparation.**
  - **This task is measurement and only measurement.** Its own «Files affected» row says *none*. There is no code to write and no test that can stand in: LCP, CLS and INP are properties of a rendered storefront with real products, real merchant images and a real network, and no runner building a theme package has one. Writing a test here would be writing a test against jsdom, which paints nothing.
  - **The byte half is already enforced and was re-measured today rather than quoted from the baseline.** `app.css` 58.8 KB · `salla-components.css` 44.7 KB · `app.js` 34.1 KB · `product.js` 2.8 KB · `home.js` 2.3 KB · `media.js` 11.7 KB · first-load JS 37.9 KB. **Every asset is inside its ceiling**; `app.css` is the single one still above its 50 KB Phase-8 target, which is T-8.01's open item and not this task's.
  - **`/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §3 is the deliverable**, and the conditions are most of it: **five runs per page and take the median**, 4× CPU throttle, Slow 4G, cold cache, a fresh profile with no extensions — an ad blocker changes every number. **Lab is not field**, and the section says so in terms: the budgets are defined at the 75th percentile of real users, Lighthouse gives one synthetic run, so a lab pass is necessary and not sufficient and a lab number must never be reported as a field number.
  - **The LCP element is stated per page in advance rather than discovered**, which turns a vague «is it fast» into a check that can fail: Home's hero, the PDP's first gallery frame, the cart's first line thumbnail — the three eager images `lint:images` already guarantees are the only eager ones. **If Lighthouse names something else, that is the finding.**
  - **The two LCP suspects are named before measuring, so the result is diagnostic rather than just a number.** ⚠ **Critical CSS is still not inlined** — T-8.01 deferred the platform's sheet but never extracted the above-fold rules, because choosing them needs a rendered page, and `app.css` at 58.8 KB still blocks first paint. **That extraction is the work T-8.01 deliberately left for a measurement to justify, and this is that measurement.** The two remaining render-blocking sheets are deliberate, and the rejected preconnect becomes worth revisiting only if the waterfall shows a slow font host — measured, rather than guessed at.
  - **CLS gets a suspect table rather than a threshold**, because 0.05 is the tightest budget in the file and «it passed» is not a useful result if a shift was visible. Seven named suspects, each with what it looks like. **The sharpest is the font swap: `font-display` cannot be set from this theme at all** — the `@font-face` rules live in the stylesheet Salla serves from `theme.font.path` — so if that shift is visible it is a platform conversation and the checklist says to record it with a screen recording rather than attempt a fix. The second sharpest is `salla-components.css`: T-8.01 deferred it on the argument that every `salla-*` element is empty until hydration and so cannot shift a layout. **§3.2 is the measurement that tests that argument**, and it should be read as such.
  - **T-8.02's deferred criterion is discharged here explicitly.** That task proved every image box is *reserved* and said plainly it could not prove nothing *moved*. §3.2 is the check it was waiting for, and the checklist says so at the point of use.
  - **INP gets eleven named interactions** rather than «try the site», with the note that a control showing no feedback within 100 ms feels broken even when the total lands under 200 ms — and that if the worst three are the search modal, quick view or the login sheet, the cause is likely a subtree being built on click rather than sitting present and inert.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — the deliverable is a procedure and a set of predictions that the measurement can falsify.

#### T-8.09 — Cross-breakpoint regression — ⚠ **PARTLY DONE 2026-08-12 — the register is built and it found one open defect; the visual regression is NOT done**
- **Objective:** Verify all four tiers from doc 10.
- **Files affected:** all
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-8.08, T-0.04
- **Acceptance criteria:** Every commerce-critical flow tested at every breakpoint (doc 10 requirement). Above 768px, verify against the five derivation rules in T-0.04 — same elements, same order, no additions, no hiding — not against an artboard, since none exists.
- **Complexity:** L
- **What was done:**
  - **This criterion is unusually machine-friendly for a visual task, and that was worth exploiting.** B4 forbids three things **at every breakpoint** — adding an element absent from mobile, reordering content, hiding content that exists on mobile. **Two of the three have a signature in the markup**: `hidden md:block` adds, `lg:hidden` removes. Neither is automatically a defect, but **every one of them is a decision, and a decision nobody wrote down is indistinguishable from an accident**.
  - **`tests/t-8.09-breakpoints.test.mjs` is a register, the same shape as T-8.07's.** It found **eight** occurrences across `src/views`. Six are in templates the theme has never adopted — and the suite **verifies that excuse against the `1.365.0` tag at run time** rather than trusting a list, so the moment one of those files is edited it stops being excusable and the test says so. Two are in theme-shadowed files.
  - **One of the two is legitimate and is now justified in writing.** `filters-trigger lg:hidden` on the listing page is B4 rule 3 working exactly as intended: the trigger opens the filters drawer, and at `from-laptop` `filters.scss` turns that drawer into a static column beside the grid. **Nothing that exists on mobile is hidden — the filters are present at both tiers**, and only the affordance for opening a drawer goes away when there is no drawer.
  - **⚠ THE OTHER IS A REAL DEFECT, FOUND BY THIS TASK, AND IT IS FOUR DEFECTS IN ONE REDUNDANT ELEMENT.** Upstream ships **two** wishlist buttons on the PDP — one over the gallery image (upstream line 120) and one down in the tags-and-social row (upstream line 274). **T-4.11 moved the first into the action row beside «أضف إلى السلة», where the artboard draws it, and left the second exactly where it was.** The consequences, all four live today:
    1. **A duplicate control.** At ≥640px the PDP carries two buttons for the same action on the same product id — two tab stops, one job.
    2. **A B4 violation.** It is `hidden sm:inline-flex`, so it is absent on mobile and appears above it — the prohibition B4 states in those words.
    3. **A hard-coded English string.** `aria-label="add to wishlist"`, in an Arabic-first store, which CLAUDE.md forbids outright.
    4. **No `aria-pressed`.** Its state is never announced — the exact WCAG 1.4.1 defect T-4.01 fixed on the product card, still present here.
    - **The fix is to delete it**, because T-4.11's heart already serves every breakpoint and the artboard draws one heart, not two. **It was not deleted here: removing a visible control is the owner's call, not a test suite's.** It is registered as `open-defect` and reported by the suite as a **`todo`** rather than a failure — so it is loud on every run (`todo 1`, where this repository has always shown `todo 0`) without blocking unrelated work. **Awaiting a ruling.**
  - **Mutation-checked:** an unregistered `hidden md:flex` and an edit to a file excused as unadopted both failed the suite.
  - **⚠ THE VISUAL REGRESSION IS NOT DONE AND IS THE LARGER HALF.** The register cannot see reordering — B4's second prohibition — nor a card that grows instead of a grid that gains columns, nor a full-bleed stretch, nor a horizontal scrollbar. **`/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §4** carries it: the four tiers at 393 · 768 · 1024 · 1280 plus 320, B4's five rules each as a checkable row, the three prohibitions, **six commerce-critical flows run end to end at every width** as doc 10 requires in those words, a slow resize through the seams because bugs live at the boundary rather than mid-tier, and all of it again in Arabic — **because a single-column mobile page hides every physical-property bug, and a multi-column layout is where they surface.**
  - **What became visible:** nothing yet — but the PDP has been carrying two wishlist buttons since T-4.11, and now that is written down.

#### T-8.10 — Merchant settings validation — ⚠ **PARTLY DONE 2026-08-12 — the wiring is verified and one open finding raised; the toggling is NOT done**
- **Objective:** Prove every exposed setting works.
- **Files affected:** `twilight.json`
- **Twilight components:** all configurable · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none
- **Theme settings:** all
- **Dependencies:** all section tasks
- **Acceptance criteria:** Every setting toggled in a real store and verified. Theme renders correctly with all optional sections disabled, and with all enabled. Defaults sensible for a fresh install.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done:**
  - **CLAUDE.md states this task's rule as a defect condition** — «Everything configurable must be a setting… **The merchant changes it, not the developer**» — and there are exactly two ways to break it silently, which are mirror images. **A setting the merchant can never set:** a template reads `theme.settings.get('x')` and `twilight.json` declares no `x`, so the customiser shows nothing, the value is forever the fallback, and **nothing errors**, because Twig returns null. **A setting that does nothing:** the manifest declares `y` and no template reads it, so the merchant finds a control, changes it, saves, and the store is identical — **which costs more trust than a missing control does.**
  - **`scripts/check-settings.mjs` catches both, plus a missing default and a missing label**, and is wired into `pnpm lint` and CI. Today: **26 settings declared and read, 6 components with 20 fields, all wired.**
  - **Reachability is what makes it honest, and modelling it was most of the work.** `components/home/` holds twenty-eight templates and a merchant can reach far fewer: a section is reachable only if it is registered in `components` or enabled through a `component-*` entry in `features`. **The theme kept every one of upstream's features and replaced upstream's six `components` with its own six**, so upstream's Brands, main-links and enhanced-slider sections are now dead templates — and a setting only they read is *correctly* absent rather than missing. Without that model the script would have reported six false defects and been switched off within a week. The one `startsWith` case is real rather than defensive: `component-featured-products` enables three templates.
  - **The script's first run produced three findings and two were its own.** **`placeholder`** is set at runtime by `master.twig` through `theme.settings.set()` — Salla's documented mechanism for a template-scoped global — and read back by `product-card.js`; it is deliberately **not** a customiser setting, because a merchant has no business choosing a placeholder image path, and declaring it would put a developer's constant in the merchant's panel. **Five `collection` fields** were reported as unlabelled: a collection is a repeater labelled per row by `item_label` («سؤال»), and its own `label` is null on purpose because the section title already names it. Both are now handled, with the reasoning inline. **Also caught: `instant_delivery_tag` is read only through `salla.config.get('theme.settings.…')` in JS**, so a scan for the Twig form alone would have called the card's delivery pill dead.
  - **⚠ ONE REAL FINDING, AND IT IS THE OWNER'S TO RESOLVE.** **`squar_photo_bg_image_size` is read by `components/home/square-photos.twig`, which IS reachable** — the theme kept `component-square-photos` in `features` — **but the theme's `twilight.json` dropped the declaration** that upstream 1.365.0 carries. So a merchant can add the section and can never change how its images are sized. Nothing breaks; the call site falls back to `contain`. **Two defensible fixes point opposite ways:** restore the declaration if the section is meant to be available, or drop `component-square-photos` from `features` if it is not in the design and keeping the feature was the oversight. **Not decided here.** It is reported on every run as an explicit open finding and **deliberately does not fail the build** — the same treatment T-8.09 gives the duplicate wishlist button, and for the same reason: **a lint script must not pick one answer by failing until somebody picks the other.** The script also fails if a resolved finding is left in the list.
  - **Mutation-checked:** four planted defects — a removed declaration for a consumed setting, an added setting nobody reads, a nulled field label, and a new undeclared call with no fallback — all four caught.
  - **⚠ THE CRITERION ITSELF IS NOT MET AND IS NOT CLAIMED.** «Every setting toggled in a real store and verified» needs a real store. **A setting can be declared, read, defaulted and labelled — and still be wired to the wrong thing.** `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §5 carries it: all 26 settings with what each should change and the failure condition for each, the six custom sections filled and then emptied, **the two extremes that are the actual criterion** — everything optional off, then everything on at maximum — and a fresh install. Three cross-links matter: #10 says to confirm the `BreadcrumbList` JSON-LD tracks the breadcrumb toggle, because schema for content absent from the page is a T-8.04 defect; #12 says to re-run the reduced-motion pass after enabling the rich toast, because that is T-8.07's known gap; #15 says to confirm the price survives the sticky bar, because T-4.12 exists for that reason.
  - **§5.4 names the sharpest fresh-install failure in plain terms:** if seed copy would embarrass a merchant who published without editing it, that is a defect — and it links the standing note that the artboards' Arabic strings are illustrative rather than final.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — but a setting can no longer be added to the manifest without being wired, or read from a template without existing.

- **Plain-language pass, 2026-08-12, raised by the project owner:** «بداية السطر» / «نهاية السطر» in the WhatsApp side setting — **correct CSS, meaningless to a merchant**, who thinks in right and left.
  - **THE BEHAVIOUR IS UNCHANGED AND THAT WAS THE POINT.** A side follows reading direction, so it mirrors between an Arabic store and an English one; the values stay `start`/`end` and the stylesheet stays logical. **Only the words changed.** `start` now reads «يمين الشاشة (يسار في المتجر الإنجليزي)» and `end` «يسار الشاشة (يمين في المتجر الإنجليزي)», and the description says the side flips with the store's language so the merchant is told rather than surprised.
  - **The whole manifest was re-read for the same class of defect**, and five more were found: `(Cover)`/`(Contain)` — raw CSS keywords — in the product gallery's fit setting; «سليدر» transliterated in two labels; «الوضع الداكن», a four-word label on a setting that **only changes Salla's trust badges** and could not be guessed from its name; «معرّف صفحة», with no hint of where a merchant finds one; and typos in two upstream labels.
  - **⚠ `footer_is_dark` was the worst of them, and its rewrite is also a correctness fix.** T-8.10's audit had already found that its body class styles nothing — its one real effect is passing `dark` to `salla-trust-badges`. The label now says exactly that, and points at the two T-8.13 colour settings for the footer's own colours. **A label that overstates what a switch does is a defect in the same way a wrong default is.**
  - **Two defects the new test found in its own first run**, which is the argument for writing it: `sticky_add_to_cart` shipped with **no description at all**, and `header_is_sticky` referred to «تثبيت الشريط الإعلاني» — a name no label in the panel carries, because T-3.03 had rewritten it. A cross-reference to a name the merchant cannot find is worse than none, because they go looking.
  - **The test pins vocabulary, not tone.** It blocks the specific terms already found, requires every switch and dropdown to carry a description, requires each `_side` setting to keep logical *values* while using plain-language *labels*, and requires every «quoted» setting name to resolve to a real label. **It cannot invent the next bad word** — that is `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` **§5.8**, which is to read the whole panel aloud as somebody who has never seen the code.
  - **What became visible:** the customiser stopped speaking CSS.
  - 15 tests.

- **Coverage audit, 2026-08-12 — the checklist had fallen behind the theme, and nothing said so.**
  - **⚠ §5.1 opened with «Today: 26 settings, all wired» while the manifest held 44.** Nobody was wrong when they wrote it; it simply stopped being true after T-8.13 added nine colours, T-3.03 five and T-3.10 four. **A checklist that states a stale total is worse than one that states none — it reads as coverage.**
  - **The eighteen WERE checked, and that is the subtler half of the finding.** §5.5, §5.6 and §5.7 cover every one of them in more depth than a §5.1 row could. But they name them **only by their Arabic labels**, so «is every setting tested?» could not be answered by searching the file — which is the only way anybody would ever ask it. Fifteen ids appeared nowhere in the document at all.
  - **§5.1 now carries an index** — id, label, and the section that checks it — so the answer is greppable, and the total is corrected.
  - **`tests/t-8.10-qa-coverage.test.mjs` makes it self-maintaining.** It fails if a declared setting is named nowhere in the checklist, if the stated total disagrees with the manifest, or if an index row points at a section heading that does not exist. It also pins the ten sections that carry a criterion this repository cannot check, because **deleting one silently turns its criterion into «done»** — and it pins the file's own «nothing here has been executed» status line, which must only ever change because a person ran the checks.
  - **What it deliberately does not do:** judge whether the check written there is a good one. A test that graded prose would fail on wording and pass on emptiness.
  - **Two more gaps closed in passing.** §2.1's keyboard flow (a) already said «tab through the announcement bar and the WhatsApp button» — but **three controls were added to that exact flow on 2026-08-12**, each changing its tab-stop count: the bar's link, its close button, and the FAB's label, which moves the accessible name off `aria-label` onto visible text. And §6.3 gained a row for **`imagesrcset` on the hero preload**, which Safari shipped late: where it is unsupported the browser falls back to `href` — deliberately one of the candidates — so the tell is **a double fetch**, not a broken page.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — a checklist stopped overstating what it covered.
  - 14 tests.

#### T-8.11 — Cross-browser and device testing — ⚠ **NOT DONE — prepared 2026-08-12; the matrix it requires has never been agreed**
- **Objective:** Verify on the real target set.
- **Files affected:** none
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** none
- **Dependencies:** T-8.09
- **Acceptance criteria:** Safari iOS, Chrome Android, Chrome and Safari desktop. Safe-area insets correct on notched devices. Target matrix agreed in advance.
- **Complexity:** M
- **What was done — nothing was tested, and nothing is claimed.**
  - **⚠ THE FIRST CRITERION IS UNMET BEFORE ANY TESTING STARTS: «target matrix agreed in advance», and no matrix has ever been agreed.** `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` §6.1 **proposes** one — the criterion's four, plus **Samsung Internet**, which is a large share of Android in this region and a distinct engine build, plus iPad, Firefox and Edge as secondary — and says in terms that a proposal is not an agreement, because **testing against a matrix nobody signed produces a pass nobody can rely on.** Recorded as **D3**.
  - **A related gap surfaced while writing it: the project has no `browserslist` at all**, so the build targets Browserslist's defaults rather than a stated set. Agreeing the matrix is the moment to write it into `package.json`, so the build's output matches the browsers actually tested. It rides with D3 because it changes build output.
  - **⚠ THE SAFE-AREA CRITERION GETS A SPECIFIC, FALSIFIABLE PREDICTION RATHER THAN A LOOK.** The theme uses `env(safe-area-inset-*)` in exactly two places — the WhatsApp FAB and the sticky add-to-cart bar. **But `master.twig`'s viewport meta has no `viewport-fit=cover`, and without that token every `env(safe-area-inset-*)` evaluates to `0px` on iOS.** So one of two things is true and only a notched iPhone can say which: **the browser is already handling it** — iOS lays the page out inside the safe area, both controls clear the home indicator on their own, and the `env()` arithmetic is harmless dead code, which is the likely outcome and a pass — **or it is not**, and the buy button sits under the home indicator.
  - **The consequence of the failure branch is spelled out, because it is not a one-line fix.** Adding `viewport-fit=cover` extends the page edge to edge on every iOS device, changing the layout of every page, and makes the `env()` values live for the first time. **It would also expose a mismatch that is dormant today:** `whatsapp-fab.scss` pairs the logical `inset-inline-end` with the **physical** `env(safe-area-inset-left)`. In Arabic those agree, because inline-end *is* the left; **in an LTR store they do not**, so a landscape notch on the right would be compensated on the wrong side. Both changes together or neither — recorded now so the fix is not attempted by halves under time pressure.
  - **§6.3 tests what actually differs between engines** rather than re-running the same flows blindly: `<dialog>` + `showModal()` is called out as **the highest-risk item on the page**, because the focus trap, `Esc`, focus return and inertness are the browser's, **the harness deliberately never simulated any of them**, and Safari was the last engine to ship it. Then `::backdrop`, `inert`, logical properties in both directions, `aspect-ratio`, the existing Safari `background-attachment` override, `:focus-visible`, iOS momentum scrolling, and Arabic glyph joining per engine.
  - **§6.4 covers what only a real device shows:** the on-screen keyboard covering the field being typed into, the **back gesture** leaving a sheet closed but the scroll lock on — T-4.18 added `popstate` handling for exactly that and it has never run on a phone — rotation losing scroll position, and viewport-height elements jumping as the iOS address bar collapses.
  - **§6.5 requires the version to be recorded**, on the grounds that «works fine» with no version is not a test result: the next Safari release makes it unverifiable.
  - **What became visible:** nothing — but the safe-area question now has a stated prediction that a single device can settle in two minutes.

#### T-8.13 — Merchant colour panel — ✅ **DONE 2026-08-12** · ⚠ **the merchant can break contrast, and that is accepted in writing as AC-13**
- **Objective:** Give the merchant control, from the theme customiser, of the colours the design fixes today: page background, header background and text, footer background and text, buttons (fill and label), icons, and primary body text.
- **Files affected:** `twilight.json`, `src/assets/styles/01-settings/global.scss`, `src/views/layouts/master.twig`, `src/locales/*.json`, `docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md`, `scripts/check-a11y.mjs`
- **Twilight components:** none · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** ten new, all `format: "color"`
- **Dependencies:** T-2.01 (the token layer), T-3.01 (`master.twig` is the only path a setting has to CSS), T-8.06 (the contrast checker this task must extend)
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  - **Every colour stays a token in `01-settings/global.scss`. The setting fills the token; it does not replace it.** No raw colour in any component, and `stylelint`'s `color-no-hex` outside the settings layer keeps it that way.
  - Every setting is emitted through the existing `:root` block in `master.twig`, which loads after `app.css` and therefore wins — the mechanism `--color-primary` and `--color-brand-secondary` already use. **A setting left unset emits nothing**, so the design's default survives untouched.
  - **The contrast table in `/docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md` is recomputed, or it is recorded explicitly that the merchant can now break contrast** — with a warning mechanism proposed if one is possible.
  - No hard-coded user-facing string; every label and description through the customiser and `src/locales/`.
- **Complexity:** M
- **⚠ The opening fact, which is why this task exists at all.** `format: "color"` is real — see the T-8.13 row in `/docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md`. T-2.01 concluded it did not exist after three sound checks, all of which looked at the wrong corpus. `secondary_color` was converted to it on 2026-08-12 ahead of this task, because that was a one-line correction to a defect and not a feature.
- **⚠ THE CENTRAL TENSION, STATED BEFORE ANY CODE.** CLAUDE.md says two things that pull against each other here: *«everything configurable must be a setting»* and *«never change typography, spacing, UX or component hierarchy from what the design specifies»*. **Colour is the one axis where the merchant can produce a page the design would not accept** — not by breaking a layout, but by making text unreadable. This task therefore cannot be judged only on «did the setting reach the token»; it has to say, per setting, what the merchant can wreck.
- **The owner ruled the full set on 2026-08-12**, including the two page-wide settings and the three the proposal recommended against: *«أنا من سيضبط الألوان، وأتحمّل نتيجة أي تغيير»*. Recorded as **AC-13**.
- **What was done:**
  - **Nine settings, seven new tokens, and not one raw colour added to a component.** Every setting fills a token `01-settings/global.scss` declares; `master.twig` emits the merchant's value into the `:root` block that already loads after `app.css` and therefore wins. The consuming files gained `var()` references and nothing else — `store-header.scss`, `footer.scss`, `04-components/buttons.scss`, `03-elements/icons.scss`.
  - **EVERY GUARD IS THE POINT, NOT THE PLUMBING.** All nine emissions are wrapped in `{% if theme.settings.get(…) %}`, so an unset setting emits no line, the token keeps its default, and **a store that touches nothing is byte-identical to the design** — the owner's second constraint. Four of the seven tokens are `var()` aliases rather than restated hexes, so no colour gained a second home to drift in.
  - **⚠ THREE SETTINGS SHIP WITH NO DEFAULT, AND THAT IS THE ONLY CORRECT ANSWER RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** `color_button_background`, `color_button_text` and `color_icons` fill tokens whose current values this theme does not own: the first two alias the merchant's own store colour, which the platform writes per store, and the third is `currentColor`. **A literal hex in any of the three would repaint every store that has already set a brand colour** — grey buttons on a store with a brand. There is a test asserting they stay empty, with that reasoning attached, because the "missing default" looks like an oversight to anyone who has not read this.
  - **Two defaults were wrong in the proposal and were corrected by reading the stylesheets rather than by assuming.** The header bar and the footer panel were proposed as `#FFFFFF`; both are **`#F7F6F4`**, the measured section panel — T-2.18 established the header as a floating rounded bar and the footer as a panel, not a white band. Shipping the proposed value would have lightened both on every store that never opened the panel.
  - **Two scoping decisions carry the weight, and each has a test.** The header colours apply to `.store-header--solid` **only**: on Home the header is transparent over the hero and its icons are white because a photograph is behind them, so a background there would paint over the design's overlay and an ink there could put dark text on a dark image. And `--surface-footer` is scoped to `.store-footer__inner.s-block__panel` rather than to `.s-block__panel`, which **every section on every page shares** — a setting labelled «التذييل» that recoloured the whole store would be a defect no correct plumbing would excuse.
  - **The button colours reach `.btn--primary` and deliberately nothing wider.** `bg-primary` also paints the cart's shipping-progress bar, a brand-page chip and a home-block disc; those are not buttons. The border moves with the fill, because upstream's `--primary` sets `border border-primary` and leaving it behind draws the old store colour as a 1px ring around the new one.
  - **⚠ THE ICON TOKEN EXISTS AGAINST T-2.04'S EXPLICIT WRITTEN ADVICE**, which said «no icon colour token exists and none should be added» — because an icon font inherits `currentColor` and so takes the colour of whatever contains it. The owner overruled it. **The reasoning was preserved rather than deleted** and the consequence is named where the next person will read it: set, it makes the overlay header's icons and any icon inside a filled button take the merchant's colour instead of their context's. Default `currentColor`, so unset it is a no-op.
  - **⚠ CONTRAST CANNOT BE WARNED ABOUT, AND FOUR MECHANISMS WERE CHECKED BEFORE THAT WAS WRITTEN DOWN.** A build-time check sees only the defaults; the platform exposes no validation hook for a setting; `color-contrast()` is not in the target browsers; and Twig cannot compute luminance — the platform's `theme.color.darker()`/`is_dark` helpers operate on the store's primary colour only, never on an arbitrary setting value. **The only channel the theme controls is the setting's own `description`**, so every one of the nine states `#RRGGBB` and, where it has a partner, the 4.5:1 it must hold against it; three additionally say what they will break. Full argument in **AC-13**.
  - **The contrast table in `/docs/DERIVED-DECISIONS.md` was not recomputed, because there is nothing new to compute — it was relabelled instead, which is the honest fix.** Every row still describes the defaults exactly. What changed is that the defaults are no longer the same thing as «the store», and the table now says so at the top rather than reading as a guarantee it can no longer make.
  - **What became visible:** the merchant sets nine of the store's colours from the theme customiser, with a colour wheel, and a store that changes none of them looks exactly as it did.
  - 48 tests. The sharpest resolves each token through its `var()` aliases to the colour it actually paints and compares that against the default offered in the customiser — the failure it catches is a default one shade off the measured value, which is invisible in review and ships to every store that never opens the panel. `/docs/MANUAL-QA.md` **§5.5** carries what they cannot: the zero test, each setting alone against a table of what must *not* move, and a deliberately hostile pass at `#CCCCCC` ink whose expected result is an unreadable store.

#### T-8.12 — Release
- **Objective:** Publish to the store.
- **Files affected:** `twilight.json`, CHANGELOG
- **Twilight components:** all · **New components:** none · **New sections:** none · **Dynamic data:** none · **Theme settings:** all
- **Dependencies:** T-8.01 → T-8.11
- **Acceptance criteria:** Salla theme review passed. Doc 17 fully signed off. Rollback plan documented. Override register (T-1.02) current so the next SDK upgrade is tractable.
- **Complexity:** M
